# Progress tracker

**Regenerated from the epic files** by
`scripts/gen_progress.py` — task ids, titles, model tiers, and effort are derived, never
hand-copied, so the two cannot drift. **Version** is derived too, from
[`RELEASES.md`](RELEASES.md); editing it here is pointless because the next regeneration
overwrites it. Only the state columns (Owner, Status, Commit,
Review, Note) are edited by hand.

The epic files in [`tasks/`](tasks/) are the source of truth for *scope*; this file
tracks *state*. If they disagree about what a task is, the epic file wins — and the
disagreement is an amendment to log.

## Status vocabulary

| Status | Means |
|---|---|
| `todo` | Not started. Check the epic's entry gate before claiming — an open gate
blocks the whole epic regardless of `Depends`. |
| `wip` | Claimed. The **Owner** column names who; one owner at a time. |
| `blocked` | Started and stopped. The **Note** column must say what blocks it. |
| `review` | Code complete, awaiting the Fable-tier review that Opus-tier and
gate-feeding tasks require. The **Review** column carries the reviewer's verdict. |
| `done` | Every definition-of-done item in runbook §4 holds, verification output
recorded, commit landed, and any required review passed. |
| `dropped` | The task's premise no longer holds, so it is resolved without being
executed. The **Note** must say who decided and why. A drop is not a deferral —
a deferred task is still `todo`. |

**A task is never `done` with a failing check.** If the suite is red it is `blocked`
and the Note says so. That rule is the whole point of this file.

## Claiming and concurrency

- **This file is canonical on `master`**, not on an epic branch — otherwise it forks per
  branch and no copy is true. Update it with a single commit touching only your row.
- **Claiming is one commit** setting Owner and moving the row to `wip`. If two agents
  race, the later commit loses and that agent picks another task — cheap, because the
  loser has done no work yet.
- **Review is tracked in the Review column**, not as separate rows. Every Opus-tier
  task and every task feeding a gate claim needs one before it can reach `done`.
- Regenerate the derived columns and the rollup with `python scripts/gen_progress.py`
  after any task is added, split, or retitled. It carries the five state columns
  through unchanged, so regenerating mid-execution is safe and never resets a claim.

**`v0.1` tagged and deployed, 2026-08-10 — the first release.** Cut from
`feat/e3-synthesis-and-reach` at `35e8668c` by `scripts/release.sh`, on a green
`gate.sh --full` (floor, retrieval eval, API e2e, Playwright, port hygiene).
The tag is written **last**, after the deployed stack answers `/health` — so it
records a running system, not a green suite. Demonstrated end to end before the
cut and recorded with its real output, gaps included, in
[`docs/releases/v0.1-demonstration.md`](../releases/v0.1-demonstration.md) on
the epic branch. The ladder for what ships next is
[`RELEASES.md`](RELEASES.md); the loop that executes it is
[`autonomous-loop.md`](autonomous-loop.md). **This is not the schema freeze** —
dev environment, disposable data, and CLAUDE.md's trigger (the first deploy
whose data must survive) is still ahead.

**Open and uncharacterised: one `frontend test` gate failure with no diagnostics
(2026-08-10, 22:47).** `./scripts/gate.sh` exited red at the `frontend test`
step. `npm test` run standalone immediately afterwards passed — 111 files, 753
tests — and the next full gate run was green, so the gate is green *through a
re-run*. **No output was captured**: the run was piped through a `grep` that
kept only the `GATE`/`FAILED` lines, and `gate.sh` streams rather than
persisting per-step logs, so the failure's actual cause is unknown and was
destroyed by the operator, not by the tooling. This is **not** the known
`activity.spec.ts` flake below — that one is Playwright e2e under
`npm run test:e2e`, and this was vitest under `npm test`, a different suite in a
different gate step. Recorded because one unexplained red in a suite that
otherwise passes 753/753 is exactly the shape the flake below took before three
waves walked past it. **Next occurrence: capture the whole step, do not grep.**

**Open, not closed: an e2e flake with a wrong explanation withdrawn (2026-08-10).**
`frontend/tests/activity.spec.ts:10` has failed on three of seven
`gate.sh --full` runs and passed on the retry each time, so the gate is green
*through a retry*. Four hypotheses were formed and refuted at the file —
cold-start module transform, a data-gated assertion, a Vite dep-optimizer
reload, and a thin expect-timeout margin; the last was shipped as a fix and
refuted by the next run, which failed again at the raised 15000ms. Waiting
longer changes nothing, so the element never appears. Narrowed to:
`activity.tsx` renders `<Tabs value={tab ?? "pipeline"}>`, so an undefined
`tab` at render time shows the pipeline panel and the asserted text never
mounts; `DayTab` has no early return and the `_authenticated` guard returns
immediately under DEMO. The open question is why `?tab=day` would be
unresolved when the page settles, and no run has ever captured the failing
DOM — `on-first-retry` traces the retry, which passes. `retain-on-failure`
and a failure screenshot are now configured so the next occurrence answers it.
84 executions, including the whole project serially three times in the gate's
own configuration, did not reproduce it. Recorded here because a flake that
three separate waves have walked past is a standing claim that the suite is
green.

These counts are **derived from the section tables below**, not maintained by hand;
E0, E2 and M1 had all drifted (recounted 2026-08-07). E2's implementation is complete —
every open item on its critical path now sits in M1.

**Recounted again 2026-08-10, and `gen_progress.py` still could not do it.**
E3 read 24 todo / 0 done against eleven `done` rows in its own table, M1's row
said 13 while its table held 16, and the Total was short by three. Every row
above is a count of the status column of every task row in the section tables.
**The first attempt at this recount was itself wrong** — it missed `TH1.17` and
`TH1.10`, which live in M1's table with an `TH` id, and published 14/2 for M1
and 276/41 for the Total. A hand-counted table presented as derived truth is
the defect this paragraph is about; it is recorded rather than quietly fixed.

**Tasks** counts rows, and every row is now one distinct id — the T2.86
duplicate was merged 2026-08-10 (one task, two dispatches; both notes preserved
on the surviving row).

Three things blocked the generator, and the first two are now fixed. `T0.18`
and the first `T2.86` row carried malformed table rows — T0.18's Review cell
quotes a ledger table and its three pipes were unescaped, which shifted the
end-anchored column window so the parser read the *status* as `G-B`. An earlier
version of this note repeated that parser error as a fact about the tracker;
T0.18's status is and was `done`. Both rows now parse.

**The third was diagnosed wrong twice, and the real cause is structural.**
`gen_progress.py` refused because `progress.md` held T2.115 while no epic file
defined it — which is true of the copy of `tasks/` sitting on `master`, and
false of the tree. T2.115's definition was written when the task was minted; it
was written **on the epic branch**, because that is where task text is edited,
while this file is canonical on `master`. So master's `tasks/` lags every branch
by the whole life of that branch, and the generator refuses on every task minted
during one. That is not a T2.115 defect and no amount of writing task text fixes
it — the generator can only be run from a tree whose `tasks/` is current, and its
output has to land here. Master's copy now carries T2.115's definition **taken
verbatim from the branch**; an earlier attempt wrote a retroactive reconstruction
into that slot instead, which would have replaced the genuine brief at merge, and
was reverted.

**RESOLVED 2026-08-10 — this paragraph used to say regenerating was destructive
and must not be done, and every clause of that is now false.** It is preserved
rather than deleted because the standing-notes carry that kept it is the same
mechanism that made it misleading: the guard faithfully preserved a claim the
same commit falsified, which is invariant 23 arriving by a new route. Read the
correction, not the claim.

The original blocker was the duplicate `T2.86` rows: regeneration keys
carried-forward state by id, so the last row won and the first row's owner,
three commit shas and completion note were destroyed with no error. That was
observed, not predicted. **The duplicate was merged into one row on 2026-08-10**
(one task, two dispatches; both notes preserved), so the id collision no longer
exists. The stale section headings named here — "E2 — Truth core (99 tasks)" and
"M1 — Measurement phase (7 tasks)" against rollup counts of 105 and 17 — were
**corrected by the first regeneration**, which is what they were waiting for.

Two things changed about regeneration itself, both on 2026-08-10. `Version` is
now derived from [`RELEASES.md`](RELEASES.md), so it is rebuilt rather than
carried. And the generator now **carries the hand-written prose** in this region
and above `## Rollup`, which it previously deleted on every run — 62 lines,
silently, with every row and count intact. Regenerating in place is now the
normal thing to do. The scratch-copy habit is still cheap insurance for a change
*to the generator*, but it is no longer required to avoid losing this file.

That is exactly the case the clause exists for ("so a lane cannot silently gate against the wrong tree's code"), and it silently said nothing. Report moved ahead of preflight; re-verified by driving: the run now prints `── resolved interpreter ── / Python: /root/repositories/darkmem/backend/.venv/bin/python` and *then* the `GATE BLOCKED` line. `GATE BLOCKED` on node_modules is the correct posture, not a failure. **F2:** the original worktree detection string-matched the parent directory against `.worktrees`, hard-coding this repo's layout; switched to `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`. **Deferred minor (recorded, not looped):** `--git-common-dir` returns a **relative** `.git` in a main checkout and an absolute path in a worktree, and the fallback does `cd "$GIT_COMMON_DIR/.."` in the caller's cwd — so a main checkout with no `.venv` would compute an empty `MAIN_CHECKOUT` and a confusing preflight message. **Unreachable in practice** (the branch runs only when the local `.venv` is missing, and the main checkout has one) and it fails loud rather than silently wrong, which is why it was recorded instead of re-opened. Verified both return forms directly. **Process note: this lane committed to `feat/e2-truth-core` in the main checkout rather than its lane** — the shell cwd resets between calls, so `cd` into a worktree does not persist. Second lane to do this today. Controller cherry-picked the commits onto the lane, reset the epic branch, and verified the worktree's leftover uncommitted copy byte-identical before clearing it | **Every lane in this epic had been unable to self-check before handing work back**, and it showed the same day: a lane reported the gate "preflight-fails in this lane worktree — no `.venv`" and hand-reimplemented the single-revision assertion instead. The gate is the only command that runs the alembic assertion, the floor, the frontend trio, benchlib and the F1 bench together — and `pyproject.toml`'s `testpaths` means plain `pytest -q` skips `scripts/benchlib/tests`, so a lane's green floor could sit over an unchecked harness. **The bench-cache half is still open**: `scripts/extraction_bench/.cache/` is also gitignored and absent in a fresh worktree, so `--algo-only` still cannot complete there — out of this task's scope, recorded for whoever owns it |

Closed with a one-statement `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, pinned by a genuinely concurrent test (two committing sessions on separate connections, held inside the reduce by an `asyncio.Barrier(2)` so both miss before either writes) — the re-reviewer re-ran the revert drill and got the original `config_pkey` violation, for the right reason. **The upsert forced a second change**: `_read_cache` had to move from `select(Config)` to `select(Config.value)`, because an ORM-loaded row in the identity map hands a later read the pre-upsert dict. Re-review tested both halves — the staleness is real but **narrower than reported** (the identity map is weak-referencing and the object dies by refcount in the real flow), so it is defensive hardening, not a live bug closed; the safe-direction argument **holds under adversarial construction**, since a stale serve requires the recorded `(hash, budget)` to equal the freshly computed pair and `community_set_hash` covers everything the prompt reads — any stale serve is therefore a byte-identical-prompt serve, and every other case recomputes. Cost is a burned reduce **per call**, not per occurrence. `max_reports` folded into the cache identity (F2); one row per project, so a non-default budget evicts the default entry — deliberate, erring toward cost rather than staleness. **The implementer refused the controller's promote-to-violation instruction on F4, and the re-review upheld the refusal**: `isolation_rules.py` defines the bar as scope breaches plus unlabelled escapes, a cold overview spending two completions is neither, and `render()` labels every counted row "a leak" — so promotion would have made the G-B number assert a crossing that never happened, permanently, to buy a guard the floor already provides (a planted second call now fails **9** tests). Scope disclaimers live in the note *strings*, which `render()` prints, so they travel with the anomaly. Recorded, not fixed: the cache key covers **data** identity, not **code** identity — a prompt or constant change serves pre-change text until each project's topic set churns — plus a duplicate-label tie-break corner at the 40-report boundary, and an unguarded `_read_cache` column-level select whose only protection is an in-code comment. | T2.24 | Consequence ranking + dispute-staleness decay | M | Opus | high | — | claude/e2-lane-h1 | done | 6ff59bc, aeda186 (merged 6f8f2b9) | fable adversarial: spec PASS all 3 clauses, quality **Approved** — 0 Critical / 0 Important / 3 Minor; 2 closed | The last actionable G-C dependency. Neither consequence input existed as a fact-level number. **Retrieval frequency** is the recency-decayed **MAX** over the fact's source memories via `fact_sources` → `memory_access_stats`: SUM rejected because it rewards having many sources, which is what corroboration already rewards (the same evidence counted twice); AVG rejected harder because adding a cold source would *lower* rank, so accumulating evidence would demote a fact down the queue. MAX is monotone under new sources. **Salience** is distinct graph-neighbour count — the `entity_relations` UNIQUE makes re-assertion append to `memory_ids`, so degree counts distinct relationships rather than mentions. Both failure modes stated: frequency measures *memory* retrieval as a proxy and is cold-start biased; salience is still a popularity measure and a hub accrues degree from incidental mentions. **The decay is a read-time rank tier, never a status or column**, and its clock is the `verified` event recording the transition *into* `disputed` — **not** the latest, because T2.09's recompute journals a contradiction-blocked hold with `before == after == disputed` on every pass, so reading the latest would refresh the clock forever and nothing would ever go stale. A dispute with no journalled onset is treated as not stale (conservative: failing to decay costs one glance, wrongly decaying hides a live dispute). Review **seeded 100k facts / 200k `fact_sources` / 50k access-stats rows across multiple event partitions** and confirmed every plan claim: leading `ix_knowledge_triples_review` scan over the open set, PK probes on the stats table, the events lateral executing for disputed rows only, 15ms, no seq scan. Fix round 1 swept frequency × scale and found Seq Scan at *every* point down to 1 row in 4,000, concluding statistics freshness decides it — and **caught its own measurement defect mid-flight**: a `DELETE`-only cleanup left dead tuples so `relpages` stayed bloated and inflated every Seq Scan estimate; switched to `TRUNCATE`, re-measured, replaced the contaminated figures in a separate commit before they shipped. **The rescope then falsified fix round 1's generalization by sweeping the axis nobody had tried — row width.** Every one of fix round 1's measurements reproduced (157 vs 185, 5113–5308 vs 5240, 14.4→2.1ms vs 13.3→2.5ms); what failed was the generalization. At fact ≈ 44 chars — still short for lifted sentences — a selective probe under wholly default settings chooses the full-index traversal, the plan fix round 1 said is never chosen. **Settled account: two regimes with a measured knife-edge.** 5%+ frequency forces a heap Seq Scan at every width (robust, the review's half); at the selective end the plans price within ~5% and the winner flips with row width and a parallel-worker step function near 3,100 heap pages — non-monotonic in width (125→traversal, 145→Seq Scan, 384→traversal). Wider rows and bigger tables push the selective case to the traversal, since the heap grows and a three-text-column index does not. All three prior measurements sat on that knife-edge. **Fix round 2 (7ec47b61) is prose only**, correcting the record in both documents and attributing the width axis to the rescope. **DONE — merged at 92b64df4.** Targeted 111 passed / 17 skipped; floor 4588 passed / 19 skipped; one alembic revision | **The disposition never moved across four accounts: with the index restored, every point — every frequency, scale, width and parallel setting — returns to the BitmapOr, so the fix is unconditionally justified and doc 08 §5.3's "degrades to a scan" was right in substance.** What moved was the mechanism, corrected by measurement three times rather than by argument. **Two deferred items are the controller's:** the schema inventory now lags by two live indexes on this table (this one and T2.11's trigram — the executor's flag-don't-edit call was right, the reconciliation is not its call), and the migration comment at `0001_baseline.py:548` still says the index fallback "is still cheaper than the heap", which this round shows is only sometimes true — recorded for the next real regeneration rather than reopening the baseline for a comment |

The real defect was one field away: pydantic drops the fraction at exactly zero microseconds and emits six digits otherwise, and `'Z'` (0x5A) sorts after `'.'` (0x2E), so `12:00:00Z` string-compares **newer** than `12:00:00.000001Z` — inverting the older/newer badges on a same-second pair. **The lane's fix beat the controller's instruction**: told to "compare parsed instants", it padded the missing fraction instead and said why — `Date.parse` resolves only to the millisecond, so it sees the two instants as *equal* and falls through to an arbitrary id tie-break. | T2.62 | `review_state` must open on the veto and clear on recovery | S | Opus | high | — | claude/e2-lane-n2 | done | f9106d4, ce57041 (merged 6b09106) | fable adversarial: spec PASS all 4 clauses, quality **Approved** — 0 Critical / 0 Important / 3 Minor; 2 Minors closed in a fix round, 3rd recorded for triage | Settles the precedence question a bare nullable TEXT column with three independent openers creates, **without a schema change**. The ruling: `review_state` is **derived** from a registered opener tuple (`REVIEW_OPENERS`, shaped like `CONTRADICTION_DETECTORS` so a not-yet-landed member cannot be plumbed past it) inside `recompute_fact`'s existing `FOR UPDATE` window — **the clear is the absence of every opener, never the negation of one**. Deliberate asymmetry: opening is unconditional (it can only *add* a queue row, so it hides nothing), clearing is applied only over a value the derivation could itself have produced, else left in place with a warning — a stale queue entry costs a glance, a wrongly cleared one costs the fact. **A human's bare clear reopens next recompute and that is correct**: doc 08 §6 gives the human three verbs (ratify/re-ground/edit) and each changes a condition the derivation honours; §6 gives no bare dismiss, because that affordance is T2.24's priority decay. No schema change needed — the `under-sourced` *label* belongs on T2.46's `demotion_pending_since`, and doc 08's own schema note says `review_state` "open marks disputed or under-sourced". The false-positive fix strips captured interpolated spans and checks the residue, **byte-for-byte the backend's own `_render` strategy**, verified live against the real producer on the disputed-true case *and* a counterpart-unrecorded shape nobody had tried; **not over-corrected**, since template-voice certainty words and template/data disagreement still fail, structurally, because the anchored regexes leave no free text outside captured groups. **The TypeScript port of `build_claim` was adjudicated acceptable as a demo-quarantined artifact** — zero imports outside `src/demo/`, no live wire path yet — **contingent** on the mirror-drift residual being stated or closed, since the guard catches fixtures drifting from the port but **never the port drifting from the producer**, and nothing in CI executes `build_claim`. The lane chose to **state** it rather than build a backend-pinned oracle hastily, arguing a cross-language oracle is not cheap to build correctly and a rushed one becomes the next over-strong guard — a judgement the re-reviewer endorsed; it is now the explicitly "matters most" entry in the guard's own *does NOT catch* list. Deferred: the demo quarantine is stated but structurally unguarded, and the walked set rests on a `*-curated.ts` filename convention nothing enforces |

Strip-before-rewrite makes refresh a fixed point (invariant 8's analogue on a text file), and content outside the markers returns byte for byte including CRLF — a writer that tidied a user's file has still edited it. **A damaged pair refuses rather than guesses**: missing, unpaired, inverted and duplicated markers all raise, naming the counts found, because every repair strategy overwrites a region the writer inferred and those bytes are the human's. Removal refuses on the same damage and takes the markers with it. Rendering refuses an item carrying a marker — the one way this could break the file it writes into. Four mutations, each a real way a writer eats a file: guessing the region (6 red), normalising outside whitespace (2), appending instead of replacing (6), leaving markers on uninstall (1). One weak assertion of my own found and fixed en route — an `or` that made the expression truthy. Verify 22 passed; floor 4762 passed, 19 skipped |

An independent certifier applied the rulebook and reached `unsupported` on every pair that rule decides — a finding about the rule, not the repair, and failure **1 of the 2** that stop the task under LABELLING.md's protocol. Gold reverted to `98fc4b49` (an uncertified gold fails `gate_eligibility`, so keeping it green would have meant weakening the eligibility test); both authored pairs are preserved verbatim in the round record so nothing needs re-authoring. **Deadlock:** the repaired gold is uncertified so no attempt can run from it, and R-B forbids an attempt on the unrepaired one. Breaking it means classifying the Additional-value disagreements, rule first then pairs — not this session's, which is contaminated. |
